## 1.109Precede

SACS 13.0 CONNECT Edition – CL

Defect: Fixed bracing tubulars do not export correctly on update to ISM repository.   
• Defect: Fixed UC label in Precede does not match UC in listing file when using the JO option.   
• Defect: Fixed Bending moment diagram for beam with concentrated moment load is incorrect   
• Defect: Fixed printing issue when “Printing all views in Selected Bin” to PDF.   
• Defect: Fixed issue storing more than 30 views.

SACS 11.3 CONNECT Edition

• Create a report of changes to fatigue parameters made during the current IFT/Precede session.   
Improvements to the way IFT/Precede handles errors during design. Precede now categorizes the errors (Brace angle to small/large, failure to find results file, etc.). Failure reasons are recorded along with the results.   
Fixed issue with Precede not calculating the weight of the “Special Launch Runner” section.   
Fixed/Enhanced labeling   
Fixed/Enhanced screen titles   
Added ability to open fatigue extract summation files in the interactive fatigue module. These files are produced by the GHBladed and SACS Wind Turbine processes.   
Fixed issue where If member group color is assigned using Member Properties in Precede, UC color is not being display correctly in the 3D Results Viewer.   
Fixed issue where plate stress units are displayed incorrectly.   
Fixed issue with the Member local transformation matrix report where the offsets were not being included.

SACS 11.2 CONNECT Edition Release Enhancements

The SACS decommissioning tool has been enhanced to calculate the sum of forces and center of forces enclosed in a volume. The selected volume can be used to “cut” a model and create a view or sub-model of the structure. The sectioned view can be saved as self-contained model file.   
Precede now has an improve user interface to manage saved views. Now saved views are displayed on a tree control on the left side of the Precede window.

SACS 11.2 CONNECT Edition Release Corrections

Precede/Joint Mesh – Fixed issue where the coordinates shown in the joint mesh window do not change with cursor position in 64bit Precede.   
Fixed Joint mesh file reading errors for JTMOPT line when mesh intensity, target plate length, and brace intersection fields are empty.   
Fixed issue exporting to ISM where segmented members are shown as one member.   
Fixed issue where any joint in the structure is moved using translate/rotate, the whole space load moved as well.   
• Fixed issue where Member labels were not drawn for Fy, KyLy or KzLz options.   
Fixed issue where reverse cones were exported incorrectly to ISM.   
Fixed issue where Member Mesh Tubular did not accurately take into account offsets at the second joint.   
Fixed issue where tapered sections and unsymmetric plate girder were not exported correctly to ISM.

SACS 11.1 CONNECT Edition Release Corrections

Fixed issue where member divide was not creating a new group.   
Fixed issues with ISM import/export for plate girders.   
Fixed “No Braces Found” issue in Joint Can review.   
The Seastate Weight feature failed to create a new load condition. A warning message that 'Load condition 'xxx' has not been defined' is now displayed when this feature is used to create a new load condition.   
• Fixed issue that the default value for the thermal coefficient was written to the model file incorrectly.

SACS 11.1 CONNECT Edition Release Enhancements

Upgraded ISM export/import to ISM 6.0.   
Integrated CONNECT Advisor

SACS 11.0.0.1 CONNECT Edition Release Enhancements

• Added the ability to graphically define mesh boundaries for use in Joint Meshing. This will automatically create a mesh input file to be used by the Joint Mesher.   
Integrated Collapse results viewing in Precede   
Added the ability to divide all members that fall on a particular Z plane. This is used for decommissioning workflows.   
Added the ability to create report of the “Load distribution Relative to Global Z” for all load conditions in a file. This is used for decommissioning workflows

• Fixed issue where Eurocode overrides where not available with "Norsok 2013/ Eurocode 3" code check option   
• Added ability to create wind areas per the API 4F wind wall definitions   
Remove restriction of the number of distribution joints for wind areas   
Added ability to import piping geometry, support information, and support reactions from AutoPIPE. Also added the ability visually connect the piping supports to the structure in Precede and export these connections and the SACS model to AutoPIPE via pipelink where a combine structure/piping analysis can be performed.

SACS 11.0.0.1 CONNECT Edition Release Enhancements

• Added the ability to handle additional joints in the AREA and WGTNS lines.   
Fixed issue where the blending for an inserted wave is not smooth and gives sudden jump in forces

SACS 10.1.0.1 CONNECT Edition Release Corrections

Corrected an issue where different results were being generated in comparison to release 5.7.1   
• Corrected an issue related to discrepancy in result values due to an un-initialized array

SACS 10.0.0.1 CONNECT Edition Release Enhancements

Wave Response enhanced to generate loading on large diameter tubular sections in accordance to McCamy and Fuchs approach.

SACS 10.0.0.1 CONNECT Edition Release Corrections

• Corrected a problem where the random wave analysis was terminating after running the first seed.   
Corrected a problem where the number of components exceeded the value set on the WAVTIM input line.

• Corrected a problem where loads were not being generated for the MST option for multiple seeds   
Corrected a problem where only the loads resulting from the final seed were generated in the OCI file when using multiple seeds   
Corrected a problem where a constrained wave was being blended over +/-3 T range.

Release V8i Select Series 4 v5.7 Corrections

Corrected problem where Wave Response was crashing on using the default number of Fourier components.

Appendix A – Details on expected differences in WAVE RESPONSE results due to compiler upgrade

Wave response has been upgraded to a new compiler. This will cause some differences in some of the results. This upgrade was done in the SACS 11.3 Release.

## 1.133Difference in number of wAve time points

As seen below the number of analysis time points are different. This analysis was for 600 secs with a 0.2 sec time interval. The previous compile calculated this to be 2999 time points as opposed to the correct 3000.

## 1.135Difference in phase angle

When calculating phase angle if the denominator is 0.0 we change it to a small number to avoid infinity. In the new compiler the denominator does not become 0.0 unless it is lesser than approximately 10E-21 while in the previous one it would be assumed to be zero for a much larger number (approximately 10E-15). This causes the difference in the phase angle. However, not much difference is seen in the results.

## 1.136Difference in max modal response time

The previous compiler could not distinguish between 0.00 and -0.00. Both were represented as positive. Now that the new compiler can do this, some max and min values (when they are so close to zero) may be different. This is expected and would not affect the general design.

| 19 | 0.002 | 31.500 | 日 | 19 | 0.002 | 31.500 |  |
| 20 | 0.000 | 32.400 | ← | 20 | -0.000 | 19.900 |  |
| 21 | 0.000 | 32.200 | → | 21 | -0.000 | 32.100 |  |



This is an analysis with a cyclic input wave of period 10 secs and wave height 6m. Since its cyclic the modal responses are also cyclic. The modal responses are maximum at time approximately 8.2, 18.2, 28.2, 38.2, 48.2, and 58.2. The values at these time points are the same but may be a bit higher or lower based on the different compilers. Hence even though it seems that the maximum is occurring at a totally different point. It is capturing the correct point and the code checks and any other calculations (e.g. fatigue) should not be drastically different.

## 1.137Difference in number of generalized force calculations

Generally, the number of generalized force calculations need not match. Different numbers just mean that a particular time step took a few more (or fewer) iterations to converge. If the results are similar it is fine.

## 1.138Difference in mudline moments ans shears

In dynamic analysis (especially time history) we iterate at each time step to achieve convergence. Since there are small difference in calculations between the new and previous compiler, convergence can occur quicker or slower based on the actual calculated values. Hence, we can see that the number of generalized force calculations are different. These slight changes also cause changes in structural velocity and acceleration calculations in each time step. Since we use the values from previous time step to calculate the results of the next time step, these differences propagate and are cumulative. Not much can be done about them as described in reference [1] and [2]. These differences should not cause huge differences in code check or fatigue damage calculations.
